Alternative (above Armor cap)	Ring of Deceitful Intent Ring of Deceitful Intent	Drop: Shade of Akama (Black Temple)

Best in Slot Trinkets for Feral Druid Tank in TBC Classic Phase 5
Using both trinket slots for defensive cooldowns was already optimal in balanced Phase 3-4 gear sets that valued both Threat and survivability equally, so it is no surprise that defensive trinkets are the way to go for Phase 5 as well, given that tank survivability will be far more important in Sunwell Plateau than in any prior Classic raid. As a standalone trinket, Shadowmoon Insignia remains the absolute best option in endgame BiS sets due to the combination of its strong passive avoidance stats plus its "Last Stand" on-use effect that can be leveraged either reactively (such as when your HP dips from a bad RNG spike) or preemptively (such as right before a tank swap transition or before soaking a burst damage mechanic like Stomp). In the second trinket slot, updated simulations show that Badge of Tenacity consistently outperforms the Battlemaster trinkets (Battlemaster's Determination / Battlemaster's Cruelty) that were recommended in Phase 3-4 when modeling the elevated incoming damage levels from Sunwell bosses. The ~12% avoidance boost provided by the Heightened Reflexes buff synergizes well with the Protector's Vigor HP buff from Shadowmoon Insignia when the two trinkets are used simultaneously, and its shorter 2-minute cooldown also comes in handy on the much longer fight lengths in Sunwell.
Rank	Item	Source
BiS	Shadowmoon Insignia Shadowmoon Insignia	Drop: Gurtogg Bloodboil (Black Temple)
BiS	Badge of Tenacity Badge of Tenacity	Depleted Badge Depleted Badge, 50 Apexis Shard Apexis Shard (Blades Edge Mountains)
Alternative	Commendation of Kael'thas Commendation of Kael'thas	Drop: Priestess Delrissa (Heroic Magisters' Terrace)
Alternative	Moroes' Lucky Pocket Watch Moroes' Lucky Pocket Watch	Drop: Moroes (Karazhan)


If you don't have access to the recommendations above, here are further options to choose from or farm:
Other Trinket Recommendations

Rank	Item	Source
Alternative	Steely Naaru Sliver Steely Naaru Sliver	Drop: M'uru / Entropius (Sunwell Plateau)]
Alternative	Battlemaster's Determination Battlemaster's Determination	30,000 Honor Points Honor Points, 20 Alterac Valley Mark of Honor Alterac Valley Mark of Honor or 75 Badge of Justice Badge of Justice
Alternative	Battlemaster's Cruelty Battlemaster's Cruelty	30,000 Honor Points Honor Points, 20 Alterac Valley Mark of Honor Alterac Valley Mark of Honor or 75 Badge of Justice Badge of Justice
Alternative	Shard of Contempt Shard of Contempt	Drop: Priestess Delrissa (Heroic Magisters' Terrace)
Alternative	Darkmoon Card: Vengeance Darkmoon Card: Vengeance	Quest:Darkmoon Furies Deck Darkmoon Furies Deck

Best in Slot Weapons for Feral Druid Tank in TBC Classic Phase 5
Pillar of Ferocity from Phase 3 remains the strongest mitigation weapon for bears even in Phase 5, and should continue to be your default weapon choice when tanking hard-hitting Sunwell Plateau raid bosses. However, the new cat DPS weapon out of Sunwell, Stanchion of Primal Instinct, is a reasonably efficient Threat/DPS swap that can be used in more aggressive gear sets for farm content, or when optimizing for a competitive parse on Warcraft Logs.
Rank	Item	Source
Best Mit Skewed	Pillar of Ferocity Pillar of Ferocity	Drop: Anetheron (Hyjal Summit)
Best Threat Skewed	Stanchion of Primal Instinct Stanchion of Primal Instinct	Drop: Grand Warlock Alythess / Lady Sacrolash (Sunwell Plateau)
Mit Skewed Alternative	Wildfury Greatstaff Wildfury Greatstaff	Zone Drop - Serpentshrine Cavern
Threat Skewed Alternative	Brutal Gladiator's Staff Brutal Gladiator's Staff	Drop: Serpentshrine Cavern Trash Mobs


If you don't have access to the recommendations above, here are further options to choose from or farm:
Other Weapon Recommendations

Rank	Item	Source
Threat Skewed Alternative	Staff of the Forest Lord Staff of the Forest Lord	Vendor: Smith Hauthaa (150 Badges of Justice)

Idols for Feral Druid Tank in Phase 5
When optimizing for overall raid value, Idol of the Raven Goddess from the Swift Flight Form quest chain remains the top Idol choice for any Feral Druid who is placed in a stacked physical DPS group. For pure tanking value, however, the Badge of Justice Badge of Justice reward Idol of Terror continues to be best in slot in Phase 5, and can be justified if your raid is wiping to tank deaths and you are already wearing mit skewed gear in every other slot.
Rank	Item	Source
Best Individually	Idol of Terror Idol of Terror	Vendor: G'eras (20 Badges of Justice)
Best for Raid DPS	Idol of the Raven Goddess Idol of the Raven Goddess	Quest:Vanquish the Raven God Vanquish the Raven God
Best for Personal TPS	Idol of the White Stag Idol of the White Stag	Drop: Supremus (Black Temple)
Alternative	Idol of Brutality Idol of Brutality	Drop: Magistrate Barthilas (Stratholme)


If you don't have access to the recommendations above, here are further options to choose from or farm:

NerdEgghead
Wowhead Classic Feral Druid Writer
Nerdegghead is an avid Classic WoW fan and theorycrafter who wrote the Feral Druid class guides for Classic through Wrath of the Lich King. He mained a warlock during TBC and Wrath, and frequented the Elitist Jerks theorycrafting forums back then as a lurker. Nerdegghead creates sim tools and stat weight calculators for tanking and cat DPS, and serves a moderator on the druid class Discord where they collaborate on these tools and help out players of all skill levels.

By Djuntas (19,188 – 3·27·122) on 2022/05/11 (Burning Crusade)	
	
Shattered Sun Pendant of Resolve Shattered Sun Pendant of Resolve Any word on this amulet from exalted vs brooch? Just copying;

"Hit exalted today (prot warrior, Scryer) so took this thing for a whirl.

It's a 45 second internal cooldown, 10 second buff and a high enough proc chance that it will proc fairly soon into the start of any fight. I saw it up a -lot-. Whilst various tests have stated it's a 45 second internal cooldown I wouldnt be surprised if it was actually 30. It seems up a -lot-.

Comparing this to the Brooch of Deftness you lose 9 hit rating and 3 Expertise, but gain 100 expertise roughly 1/5 of the time. Also, this expertise tends to come very early at the start of a fight, as the proc will go off within the first few swings of a pull, which means the expertise is there when you want it - getting that initial threat, rather than succumbing to the hell of a string of parries within the first vital few seconds.

As a result, although it likely averages out as being 20 passive expertise, I think it's more useful than that, because you get a huge whack of expertise more often when you really need it. Provided you have enough hit on other gear to not worry about losing a bit from the Brooch, I'd say this is -definitely- worth using if you're a Scryer over the Brooch. I destroyed my beloved Brooch of Deftness today after some good testing of this thing. It's awesome."

Thanks for all the updates throughout TBC.
Last edited by Djuntas on 2022/05/11

Ontheluna on 2022/05/07 (Burning Crusade)
1. Even in a mitigation set, using Gift of Arthas as your Guardian Elixir is optimal. The raid damage gain from the debuff being applied to the boss is more valuable than the marginal stat increases you can pick up instead of 30 resilience from gear.

1

	

Ontheluna on 2022/05/07 (Burning Crusade)
2. Agi has more value than hit in mit sets because of the dodge. The assumption in a mit set, particularly for SWP, is that you'll have so much rage to spend from being hit that your threat generation will be more than adequate even if a few more of your attacks miss. Haste and the crit from agi helps a small amount too.

0

	

bampie on 2022/05/07 (Burning Crusade)
Thank you for clarification. I'm curious how thread generation will be in SWP with so low hit/expertise levels, especially shortly after the pull.

0

	

NerdEgghead <Guide Author> on 2022/05/07 (Burning Crusade)
As I discussed in the embedded gear set section of this guide, you actually have a higher success rate on your abilities when wearing mit skewed SWP gear than you do when wearing balanced Phase 3-4 gear. You'll have a lot less Hit Rating but far more Expertise Rating than you ever have in the past, leading to an overall lower failure rate on pulls.

You have to taunt Brutallus quite often and no expertise is useful for that. I have seen some who have even socketed Hit Rating for it. I think that was not sufficiently included in the guide here. Multiple missed taunts can kill a tank just as easily. Also you can even taunt large amounts of threat more reliably based on the 130% range threat rule.
